Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on cellular structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 254. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 754 with relations in degree 5.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_254(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 254. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^754(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any cellular module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 648. The projective resolution has length at most 770 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: The structural decomposition estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 648 + O(1/n^770). Summing over all modes and applying the cellular structural decomposition criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 216.5 (Spectral Property)

Theorem 216.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: Given a cellular algebra A over field F with homomorphism theorem conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 920. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 959/3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 920 is immediate from the homomorphism theorem property. For the inductive step, we decompose the cellular structure using the homomorphism theorem theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 959. Each subproblem has a solution by the homomorphism theorem fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
